- Active motifs: visual or intellectual seeing `ر ء ي:B001/m01`; directed observation `ن ظ ر:B001/m01`; visible presentation `ر ء ي:B012/m01`; mutually facing appearance `ر ء ي:B004/m01`; witnessed presence `ش ه د:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ر ء ي` 83:32; `ن ظ ر` 83:23,35; `ش ه د` 83:21.
- Synthesis: Direction organizes the gaze, display makes the object available, and mutual facing converts solitary perception into a shared encounter.

#### Subchannel B. Eye, Brow, Pupil, and Reflected Person
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: The eye is treated as an assembled visual organ whose pupil carries a small reflected human image.
- Active motifs: seeing eye `ع ي ن:B001/m01`; pupil or seat of sight `ن ظ ر:B008/m01`; human image in the pupil `ء ن س:B005/m01`; eyebrow `ح ج ب:B005/m01`; glaring or protruding eye `ج ح م:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ع ي ن` 83:28; `ن ظ ر` 83:23,35; `ء ن س` 83:2,6 `ٱلنَّاس`; `ح ج ب` 83:15; `ج ح م` 83:16.
- Synthesis: The brow frames the organ, the pupil locates sight, and the reflected person makes vision reciprocal: the observer's eye visibly contains the one observed.

#### Subchannel C. Blind Eye and Occluded Vision
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: The eye remains bodily present while access to sight is blocked or covered.
- Active motifs: blindness as a covered faculty `ح ج ب:B009/m01`; intact but sightless eye `ق و م:B021/m01`; hiddenness from view `ض ل ل:B002/m01`; barrier preventing access `ح ج ب:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ح ج ب` 83:15; `ق و م` 83:6; `ض ل ل` 83:32.
- Synthesis: The organ persists, but the visual path does not. Covering and hiddenness describe loss of access rather than disappearance of the eye itself.

#### Subchannel D. Dream, Apparition, and Unstable Appearance
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A human image appears in sleep or as an uncanny presence whose reality cannot be secured by ordinary sight.
- Active motifs: dream vision `ر ء ي:B003/m01`; apparition or spirit-companion `ر ء ي:B008/m01`; human presence opposed to the uncanny `ء ن س:B001/m01`; pupil image `ء ن س:B005/m01`; inescapable entrapment `ر ي ن:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ر ء ي` 83:32; `ء ن س` 83:2,6 `ٱلنَّاس`; `ر ي ن` 83:14.
- Synthesis: Dream and apparition imitate ordinary appearance without granting ordinary verification. The reflected human image supplies a visual analogy, while entrapment captures the apparition's grip on the perceiver.

#### Subchannel E. Harmful Gaze and Bodily Impact
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A gaze is believed to strike another person, leaving a visible defect or inward injury.
- Active motifs: injurious evil eye `ع ي ن:B004/m01`; harmful force issuing from a person `ن ف س:B003/m01`; injury reaching the heart `ق ل ب:B016/m01`; discoloring defect left by a look `ن ظ ر:B007/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ع ي ن` 83:28; `ن ف س` 83:26; `ق ل ب` 83:14,31; `ن ظ ر` 83:23,35.
- Synthesis: The gaze functions as an agent, the person supplies its force, and the victim's body or color records the effect. Perception thus reverses into causation.

#### Subchannel F. Watcher, Spy, and Surveillance
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An appointed observer watches continuously, gathers hidden information, and reports it to authority.
- Active motifs: watcher preserving a trust `ن ظ ر:B009/m01`; spy as an observing eye `ع ي ن:B005/m01`; vigilant alertness `ح ف ظ:B004/m01`; supervisory control `س ط ر:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ن ظ ر` 83:23,35; `ع ي ن` 83:28; `ح ف ظ` 83:33; `س ط ر` 83:13.
- Synthesis: Vigilance sustains attention, the spy crosses the boundary between visible and hidden information, and supervision turns observation into institutional control.

#### Subchannel G. Knowledge, Recognition, and Evidential Judgment
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A trace is perceived, interpreted, and converted into recognition, knowledge, or warranted certainty.
- Active motifs: practical knowledge `د ر ي:B001/m01`; disclosed knowledge `ع ل م:B001/m01`; recognition from a trace `ع ر ف:B003/m01`; certainty following an indication `ظ ن ن:B001/m01`; informed testimony `ش ه د:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `د ر ي` 83:8,19; `ع ل م` 83:6; `ع ر ف` 83:24; `ظ ن ن` 83:4; `ش ه د` 83:21.
- Synthesis: The trace initiates inference, recognition identifies the object, and testimony makes the resulting knowledge available to others. Certainty is the endpoint of interpreted evidence.

#### Subchannel H. Suspicion, Divination, and Hidden Knowledge
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Uncertain signs are read by a specialist who claims access to concealed outcomes, sometimes through animal omens.
- Active motifs: doubt and conjecture `ظ ن ن:B003/m01`; untrustworthy outcome `ظ ن ن:B006/m01`; diviner claiming hidden knowledge `ع ر ف:B012/m01`; specialist or learned sage `ر ب ب:B003/m01`; raptor used within the omen field `ع ل م:B006/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ظ ن ن` 83:4; `ع ر ف` 83:24; `ر ب ب` 83:6,15; `ع ل م` 83:6.
- Synthesis: Uncertainty creates demand for interpretation, the specialist claims to resolve it, and the raptor supplies a mobile sign whose behavior can be read as an omen.

#### Subchannel I. Memory, Inner Retention, and Forgetting
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Perceived or spoken content is held in the mind, rehearsed inwardly, or lost through forgetting.
- Active motifs: stable retention in memory `ح ف ظ:B002/m01`; loss of remembered content `ض ل ل:B004/m01`; inner unspoken statement `ق و ل:B012/m01`; mind and inward awareness `ن ف س:B013/m01`; reflective opinion `ر ء ي:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ح ف ظ` 83:33; `ض ل ل` 83:32; `ق و ل` 83:13,17,32; `ن ف س` 83:26; `ر ء ي` 83:32.
- Synthesis: Attention admits content, inward speech rehearses it, retention keeps it available, and forgetting breaks the link between prior perception and present recall.

### 12. Living Body, Reproduction, Injury, and Recovery
- Semantic invariant: Life is embodied through breath, organs, bodily proportion, reproduction, vulnerability, and restoration.
- Surface relation: mixed; 83:14 names hearts, 83:24 faces, and 83:26 the self, while the lexical field expands into detailed anatomy and medicine.
- Surprising reach: The body channel includes diaphragm, pelvis, lip cleft, mustache and throat, lochia, afterbirth, animal presentation, nausea, heart injury, and medicinal repair.

#### Subchannel A. Heart, Lung, Breath, and Life
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Breath moves through the chest, lungs sustain respiration, and the heart stands as the inward center of embodied life.
- Active motifs: heart or inward organ `ق ل ب:B001/m01`; lung and pulmonary distress `ر ء ي:B009/m01`; exhaled breath `ن ف س:B001/m01`; living self `ن ف س:B011/m01`; diaphragm or inner partition `ح ج ب:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ق ل ب` 83:14,31; `ر ء ي` 83:32; `ن ف س` 83:26; `ح ج ب` 83:15.
- Synthesis: The diaphragm partitions the chest, the lung moves breath across that interior, and heart and living self supply the vital center whose failure would end the process.

#### Subchannel B. Constitution, Physique, and Bodily Proportion
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A body is composed according to temperament, bulk, bone, stature, and proportional balance.
- Active motifs: bodily temperament and composition `م ز ج:B002/m01`; bodily mass and size `ج ر م:B007/m01`; main bulk of a thing `ع ظ م:B002/m01`; hard bone `ع ظ م:B005/m01`; stature and bodily frame `ق و م:B011/m01`; proportionate formation `و ز ن:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `م ز ج` 83:27; `ج ر م` 83:29; `ع ظ م` 83:5; `ق و م` 83:6; `و ز ن` 83:3.
- Synthesis: Temperament describes internal mixture, bone and bulk provide material structure, and stature displays how those components have been proportioned into a body.

#### Subchannel C. Mouth, Lip, Tongue, and Tooth
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: The mouth is assembled from lip, tongue, tooth, mustache, and throat passages that jointly shape expression and intake.
- Active motifs: cleft in the upper lip `ع ل م:B004/m01`; inverted or deformed lip `ق ل ب:B012/m01`; tongue as articulator `ق و ل:B002/m01`; mustache and throat channels `ش ر ب:B006/m01`; visible tooth `ض ح ك:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ع ل م` 83:6; `ق ل ب` 83:14,31; `ق و ل` 83:13,17,32; `ش ر ب` 83:28; `ض ح ك` 83:29,34.
- Synthesis: Lip and tooth frame the opening, tongue shapes outgoing speech, and throat channels regulate what passes inward. Visible expression and ingestion share the same bodily threshold.

#### Subchannel D. Chest, Pelvis, Flank, and Back
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: The torso is mapped through chest, pelvic heads, flank, lower back, and variations in bodily thickness.
- Active motifs: chest `ك ل ل:B007/m01`; heads of the pelvic bones `ح ج ب:B006/m01`; flank or waist `ق ر ب:B015/m01`; lower back and tail-root `ص ل ي:B006/m01`; short thick build `ك ل ل:B008/m01`; enlarged buttock pad `ع ظ م:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ك ل ل` 83:12; `ح ج ب` 83:15; `ق ر ب` 83:21,28; `ص ل ي` 83:16; `ع ظ م` 83:5.
- Synthesis: The motifs form a contiguous body map from chest through waist and pelvis to the lower back. Thickness and artificial enlargement describe how that frame can vary in profile.

#### Subchannel E. Pregnancy, Birth, Afterbirth, and Nursing
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Gestation approaches delivery, the young presents in a particular orientation, and membranes and afterbirth accompany emergence and early care.
- Active motifs: approaching parturition `ق ر ب:B012/m01`; visible pregnancy `ر ء ي:B010/m01`; lochia and childbirth discharge `ن ف س:B005/m01`; afterbirth accompanying the newborn `ش ه د:B006/m01`; forelimbs-first animal presentation `و ج ه:B010/m01`; birth membrane on the young `م س ك:B010/m01`; womb's holding bond `م س ك:B009/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ق ر ب` 83:21,28; `ر ء ي` 83:32; `ن ف س` 83:26; `ش ه د` 83:21; `و ج ه` 83:24; `م س ك` 83:26.
- Synthesis: Pregnancy becomes visible, nearness signals labor, presentation governs the mechanics of delivery, and membranes and discharge mark the transition from gestation to newborn care.

#### Subchannel F. Disease, Nausea, and Bodily Injury
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Illness disturbs internal mixture, movement, or an organ and may culminate in a direct strike to the heart or face.
- Active motifs: bodily nausea `ر ي ن:B004/m01`; bitter bodily humor `م ر ر:B007/m01`; heart disease `ق ل ب:B011/m01`; persistent pain in a limb `ق و م:B019/m01`; contagious disease `ع د و:B006/m01`; injury to the heart `ق ل ب:B016/m01`; harmful draught `س ق ي:B010/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ر ي ن` 83:14; `م ر ر` 83:30; `ق ل ب` 83:14,31; `ق و م` 83:6; `ع د و` 83:12; `س ق ي` 83:25.
- Synthesis: Nausea and bitter humor describe internal disturbance, limb pain and disease alter bodily function, and the heart-strike gives the channel an acute traumatic endpoint.

#### Subchannel G. Wound Repair, Convalescence, and Relief
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A wound settles, a thick medicinal preparation repairs tissue or leather, and the patient rises out of illness into relief.
- Active motifs: healed and quieted wound `ء ر ك:B005/m01`; thick medicinal or repair syrup `ر ب ب:B006/m01`; recovery from illness `ب ل ل:B003/m01`; recovery after childbirth or disease `ع ل و:B011/m01`; release from distress `ن ف س:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ء ر ك` 83:23,35; `ر ب ب` 83:6,15; `ب ل ل` 83:14 `بَلْ`; `ع ل و` 83:18,19; `ن ف س` 83:26.
- Synthesis: Repair begins with a material preparation, continues as swelling and corruption subside, and ends when the body regains its prior level and distress opens into relief.

#### Subchannel H. Viscera, Flank, and Soft Abdominal Tissue
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: The abdomen is mapped through entrails, diaphragm, flank, and loose external tissue.
- Active motifs: intestines `م ر ر:B008/m01`; loose abdominal flesh `ط ف ف:B008/m01`; inner diaphragm `ح ج ب:B004/m01`; flank and waist `ق ر ب:B015/m01`; rendered body fat `ء ه ل:B006/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `م ر ر` 83:30; `ط ف ف` 83:1; `ح ج ب` 83:15; `ق ر ب` 83:21,28; `ء ه ل` 83:31.
- Synthesis: The diaphragm marks the upper interior, entrails occupy the visceral center, flank locates the side wall, and loose tissue and fat describe the abdomen's outer material.

### 13. Water, Drinking, and Vessel Technology
- Semantic invariant: Water becomes usable through source, allocation, drinking, mixture, storage, and maintenance of containers.
- Surface relation: direct; 83:25-28 presents being given drink, a sealed beverage, admixture, a spring, and drinking.
- Surprising reach: The liquid scene expands into wells, reservoirs, throat passages, leather tanning, leaking skins, and bitter survival water.

#### Subchannel A. Drinking, Share, and Draught
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: Water is allocated as a share, presented at a drinking place, and consumed in successive draughts.
- Active motifs: consuming liquid `ش ر ب:B001/m01`; allotted share of water `ش ر ب:B002/m01`; drinking group `ش ر ب:B003/m01`; drinking place or vessel `ش ر ب:B004/m01`; a single draught `ن ف س:B006/m01`; giving drink `س ق ي:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ش ر ب` 83:28; `ن ف س` 83:26; `س ق ي` 83:25.
- Synthesis: Allocation determines who drinks, the vessel or place stages access, and successive draughts turn a communal resource into embodied refreshment.

#### Subchannel B. Spring, Well, Reservoir, and Water-Holding Ground
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Water emerges, gathers in a deep or stable place, and is retained for use.
- Active motifs: flowing spring `ع ي ن:B006/m01`; well or water pit `ق ل ب:B007/m01`; deep abundant water `ع ل م:B005/m01`; accumulated fresh water `ر ب ب:B013/m01`; water-retaining ground or well lining `م س ك:B004/m01`; fissure releasing water `ف ج ر:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ع ي ن` 83:28; `ق ل ب` 83:14,31; `ع ل م` 83:6; `ر ب ب` 83:6,15; `م س ك` 83:26; `ف ج ر` 83:7.
- Synthesis: Fissure creates flow, terrain gathers it, and stable ground holds it. Spring, well, and reservoir are successive spatial forms of accessible water.

#### Subchannel C. Waterskin, Leak, Tanning, and Repair
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A hide is made into a water container, lightly tanned, tested for leaks, and sealed by wetting its seams.
- Active motifs: waterskin container `ق ر ب:B009/m01`; hide retaining contents `م س ك:B005/m01`; thin or leaking point in a skin `ع ي ن:B007/m01`; conditioning and sealing a waterskin `ش ر ب:B013/m01`; small tanning application `ن ف س:B007/m01`; thick repair compound `ر ب ب:B006/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: `ق ر ب` 83:21,28; `م س ك` 83:26; `ع ي ن` 83:28; `ش ر ب` 83:28; `ن ف س` 83:26; `ر ب ب` 83:6,15.
- Synthesis: Tanning creates a durable hide, inspection locates the weak point, wet clay or thick compound closes the stitching, and the finished skin becomes a portable water store.

#### Subchannel D. Chosen Drink and Admixture
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A selected clear drink receives an admixture that changes its flavor, body, or effect.
- Active motifs: choice clear nectar `ر ح ق:B001/m01`; mixing one liquid with another `م ز ج:B001/m01`; liquid consumed `ش ر ب:B001/m01`; sustaining mixture `م س ك:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ر ح ق` 83:25; `م ز ج` 83:27; `ش ر ب` 83:28; `م س ك` 83:26.
- Synthesis: Selection establishes purity, admixture modifies the chosen base, and drinking completes the transformation from prepared liquid to experienced taste and effect.

#### Subchannel E. Bitter or Harmful Water
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A drinker faces water that is bitter, repellent, or barely sufficient to sustain life.
- Active motifs: reluctantly consumed water `ش ر ب:B005/m01`; bitterness opposed to sweetness `م ر ر:B003/m01`; water just sufficient for life `ن ف س:B008/m01`; a hateful drink striking the heart `س ق ي:B010/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ش ر ب` 83:28; `م ر ر` 83:30; `ن ف س` 83:26; `س ق ي` 83:25.
- Synthesis: Scarcity makes the drink necessary, bitterness makes it resistant to desire, and bodily harm turns hydration into ordeal.

### 14. Cultivation, Fruit, and Prepared Food
- Semantic invariant: Plant life moves from covered seed through watering and ripening to fruit, sweetness, and processed nourishment.
- Surface relation: indirect; the sealed and mixed drink at 83:25-28 activates lexical branches of seed-covering, first watering, fruit enclosure, honey, syrup, and extract.
- Surprising reach: The channel includes a complete arak-tree ecology, grain color change, palm calyx opening, rendered fat, and medicinal food preparation.

#### Subchannel A. Covered Seed and First Watering
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Seed is covered in soil, receives its first irrigation, and opens into growth.
- Active motifs: covering seed `ك ف ر:B008/m01`; first watering of a crop `خ ت م:B004/m01`; allocated irrigation channel `س ق ي:B003/m01`; fissure or channel releasing water `ف ج ر:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ك ف ر` 83:34,36; `خ ت م` 83:25,26; `س ق ي` 83:25; `ف ج ر` 83:7.
- Synthesis: Covering places seed in its growth medium, irrigation brings controlled water, and the opened channel converts stored water into germination.

#### Subchannel B. Grain, Ear, and Ripening Color
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Grain rises into an ear and changes color as it matures toward harvest.
- Active motifs: wheat or grain `ب ر ر:B006/m01`; ear shifting from green toward yellow `م ز ج:B004/m01`; clear crop color `ج ر م:B009/m01`; cultivated plant growth `ر ب ب:B012/m01`; raised plant head `س ن م:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ب ر ر` 83:18,22; `م ز ج` 83:27; `ج ر م` 83:29; `ر ب ب` 83:6,15; `س ن م` 83:27.
- Synthesis: Growth raises the ear, color records maturation, and the visible change marks when grain is approaching harvest.

#### Subchannel C. Palm Heart, Fruit Enclosure, and Opening Calyx
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A plant's inner heart develops fruit inside a cover until the calyx opens and reveals ripeness.
- Active motifs: palm or tree heart `ق ل ب:B003/m01`; enclosing fruit calyx `ك ف ر:B010/m01`; opening date spathe `ض ح ك:B007/m01`; red unripe date `ق ل ب:B015/m01`; desirable fruit `ف ك ه:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ق ل ب` 83:14,31; `ك ف ر` 83:34,36; `ض ح ك` 83:29,34; `ف ك ه` 83:31.
- Synthesis: The inner heart produces, the calyx protects, opening exposes, and changing fruit color signals the path from formation to edible yield.

#### Subchannel D. Arak Tree, Shrub, Fruit, and Green Ground
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Arak and related shrubs grow in green ground, bear fruit, and provide useful wood and forage.
- Active motifs: arak tree and toothstick wood `ء ر ك:B001/m01`; leafy alāʾ shrub `ء ل ي:B005/m01`; arak fruit `ب ر ر:B007/m01`; green watered ground `ش ر ب:B014/m01`; wooden cup or timber `ن ض ر:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ء ر ك` 83:23,35; `ء ل ي` 83:31 `إِلَىٰ`; `ب ر ر` 83:18,22; `ش ر ب` 83:28; `ن ض ر` 83:24.
- Synthesis: Moist ground supplies the habitat, shrub and tree provide layered vegetation, fruit supplies food, and wood carries the plant into human use.

#### Subchannel E. Honey, Wax, Extract, and White Sweetness
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Honey is held in wax, extracted with oily residue, and recognized by sweetness and pale color.
- Active motifs: honey in wax `ش ه د:B007/m01`; honey or sweet mixture `م ز ج:B003/m01`; fatty extract or pressed juice `ك س ب:B004/m01`; white sweet substance or foam `ض ح ك:B009/m01`; selected nectar `ر ح ق:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ش ه د` 83:21; `م ز ج` 83:27; `ك س ب` 83:14; `ض ح ك` 83:29,34; `ر ح ق` 83:25.
- Synthesis: Wax contains the honey, extraction separates usable sweetness, and color and taste identify the refined product as a chosen nectar.

#### Subchannel F. Thickening, Rendering, and Repair Paste
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Liquid or fat is heated or left to thicken into food, condiment, medicine, or repair paste.
- Active motifs: liquid curdling at its final stage `ء و ل:B005/m01`; thick syrup or oil sediment `ر ب ب:B006/m01`; rendered fat `ء ه ل:B006/m01`; mixing liquids `م ز ج:B001/m01`; bitter flavor `م ر ر:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ء و ل` 83:13; `ر ب ب` 83:6,15; `ء ه ل` 83:31; `م ز ج` 83:27; `م ر ر` 83:30.
- Synthesis: Admixture begins the preparation, thickening changes texture, rendering concentrates fat, and taste determines whether the product functions as food, condiment, or medicine.

#### Subchannel G. Cutting, Harvest, and Dry Remnants
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Fruit or grain is cut from the plant, gathered, and followed by gleaning of fallen or dried remnants.
- Active motifs: cutting fruit or crop `ج ر م:B001/m01`; fallen dates and dry harvest remnants `ج ر م:B002/m01`; grain crop `ب ر ر:B006/m01`; desirable fruit `ف ك ه:B002/m01`; acquired yield `ك س ب:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ج ر م` 83:29; `ب ر ر` 83:18,22; `ف ك ه` 83:31; `ك س ب` 83:14.
- Synthesis: Cutting separates produce from plant, gathering secures the main yield, and gleaning recovers what remains after the first harvest.

#### Subchannel H. Seasonal Growth and Plant Positioning
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Summer vegetation emerges after spring, and a cultivator positions tender fruit or vines to protect and direct growth.
- Active motifs: summer forage growth `ع د و:B011/m01`; cultivated vegetation `ر ب ب:B012/m01`; positioning a cucumber or melon plant `و ج ه:B012/m01`; raised plant head `س ن م:B003/m01`; green watered ground `ش ر ب:B014/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ع د و` 83:12; `ر ب ب` 83:6,15; `و ج ه` 83:24; `س ن م` 83:27; `ش ر ب` 83:28.
- Synthesis: Season controls emergence, moist ground sustains it, and deliberate positioning guides the plant's body as fruit enlarges.

### 15. Herd, Pasture, and Animal Husbandry
- Semantic invariant: Domestic animals are grouped, fed, watered, tethered, bred, milked, and protected from loss or disease.
- Surface relation: indirect; 83:22,24 uses the `ن ع م` root and 83:28 drinking, while lexical senses disclose a pastoral system.
- Surprising reach: The system includes arak-grazing camels, abdominal illness, herd ruin, pasture plants, tether construction, milk continuity, and prepartum change.

#### Subchannel A. Herd Formation and Managed Movement
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Livestock are assembled as a herd and moved in successive groups under human management.
- Active motifs: livestock wealth `ن ع م:B005/m01`; organized herd `ر ب ب:B014/m01`; animals sent in successive groups `ر س ل:B005/m01`; stray animal separated from ownership `ض ل ل:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ن ع م` 83:22,24; `ر ب ب` 83:6,15; `ر س ل` 83:33; `ض ل ل` 83:32.
- Synthesis: Herding converts many animals into a managed unit, successive movement controls their travel, and the stray marks failure of that managed relation.

#### Subchannel B. Camel Grazing at Arak and Veterinary Distress
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Camels browse arak and its fruit, develop abdominal distress from overconsumption, and may contribute to wider livestock loss.
- Active motifs: camels habituated to arak grazing `ء ر ك:B002/m01`; arak fruit `ب ر ر:B007/m01`; abdominal illness from arak `ء ر ك:B006/m01`; communal ruin through livestock death `ر ي ن:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ء ر ك` 83:23,35; `ب ر ر` 83:18,22; `ر ي ن` 83:14.
- Synthesis: Tree and fruit supply forage, habituated browsing intensifies exposure, and abdominal illness links a concrete feeding practice to the economic danger of herd loss.

#### Subchannel C. Pasture, Watering, and Tether
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Animals feed on pasture, are brought to water, and are secured with a twisted neck rope.
- Active motifs: camel forage grass `ص ل ي:B010/m01`; green watered pasture `ش ر ب:B014/m01`; night approach to water `ق ر ب:B008/m01`; neck tether `ش ر ب:B008/m01`; tightly twisted rope `م ر ر:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ص ل ي` 83:16; `ش ر ب` 83:28; `ق ر ب` 83:21,28; `م ر ر` 83:30.
- Synthesis: Pasture supplies feed, approach to water organizes movement, and the rope controls the animal at the point where feeding and watering make it most active.

#### Subchannel D. Gestation, Milk Flow, and Early Care
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Pregnancy alters the animal's body and milk, birth occurs, and flowing milk sustains the young.
- Active motifs: newly delivered ewe `ر ب ب:B009/m01`; approaching animal birth `ق ر ب:B012/m01`; prepartum milk `ف ك ه:B004/m01`; continuous milk flow `ر س ل:B006/m01`; milk drying or failing `ك ذ ب:B006/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ر ب ب` 83:6,15; `ق ر ب` 83:21,28; `ف ك ه` 83:31; `ر س ل` 83:33; `ك ذ ب` 83:10-12,17.
- Synthesis: Gestation changes milk before delivery, birth initiates the nursing interval, and continuity or failure of flow determines whether early nourishment succeeds.

#### Subchannel E. Hump, Mounting, and Pack Preparation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A camel or horse is selected, fitted at the back and flank, mounted, and prepared to carry a rider or load.
- Active motifs: camel hump `س ن م:B002/m01`; mounting the back `س ن م:B007/m01`; prepared horse or camel `ق ر ب:B013/m01`; saddle wood `ع ظ م:B009/m01`; flank where tack and rider meet `ق ر ب:B015/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `س ن م` 83:27; `ق ر ب` 83:21,28; `ع ظ م` 83:5.
- Synthesis: The back and hump provide the carrying surface, saddle wood distributes load, and mounting turns a managed animal into transport.

### 16. Wildlife, Hunting, and Animal Signals
- Semantic invariant: Wild animals occupy habitats, move as predator or prey, emit identifying signals, and enter human hunting sequences.
- Surface relation: indirect; lexical branches of `ع ل م`, `ن ع م`, `د ر ي`, `ك س ب`, and `ص ل ي` open a wildlife scene from roots occurring at 83:6,14,16,22,24.
- Surprising reach: The field joins ibex, hyena, wolf, raptor, ostrich, traps, stalking, animal cries, and collective scattering.

#### Subchannel A. Mountain Refuge and Predator-Prey Field
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Mountain game seeks high refuge while hyena and wolf occupy the same predator-prey landscape.
- Active motifs: mountain ibex `ء و ل:B009/m01`; male hyena `ع ل م:B007/m01`; wolf `ق ل ب:B013/m01`; dry open terrain `ب ر ر:B005/m01`; raised refuge `س ن م:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ء و ل` 83:13; `ع ل م` 83:6; `ق ل ب` 83:14,31; `ب ر ر` 83:18,22; `س ن م` 83:27.
- Synthesis: Height offers refuge to prey, open terrain exposes movement, and the named predators give the landscape its pursuit relation.

#### Subchannel B. Raptor, Ostrich, and Bird Cry
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Distinct birds are recognized by flight, body form, and call across open country.
- Active motifs: hunting raptor `ع ل م:B006/m01`; ostrich `ن ع م:B006/m01`; bird and animal calls `ب ل ل:B010/m01`; repeated vocal signal `ث و ب:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ع ل م` 83:6; `ن ع م` 83:22,24; `ب ل ل` 83:14 `بَلْ`; `ث و ب` 83:36.
- Synthesis: Form and flight identify the bird at distance, while repeated call supplies an auditory marker when sight is interrupted.

#### Subchannel C. Stalking, Hunting Animal, and Trap
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A hunter conceals approach, employs trained animals, and drives prey toward a snare.
- Active motifs: concealed stalking `د ر ي:B003/m01`; hunting animals or raptors `ك س ب:B003/m01`; traps and snares `ص ل ي:B005/m01`; alternating pursuit `ع د و:B008/m01`; prey passing through terrain `م ر ر:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `د ر ي` 83:8,19; `ك س ب` 83:14; `ص ل ي` 83:16; `ع د و` 83:12; `م ر ر` 83:30.
- Synthesis: Concealment protects the approach, trained animals apply pressure, pursuit channels the prey, and the trap closes the sequence through capture.

#### Subchannel D. Flight, Dispersal, and Collective Motion
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A flock or group erupts from stillness, scatters in multiple directions, and continues in rapid movement.
- Active motifs: ostrich flight and group dispersal `ن ع م:B008/m01`; collective rush `ب ع ث:B004/m01`; sudden outpouring `ف ج ر:B003/m01`; confused commotion `ب ل ل:B009/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ن ع م` 83:22,24; `ب ع ث` 83:4; `ف ج ر` 83:7; `ب ل ل` 83:14 `بَلْ`.
- Synthesis: Sudden activation breaks the group's prior cohesion, commotion obscures ordered direction, and dispersal turns one gathered body into many moving trajectories.

### 17. Horse Racing, Gait, and Tack
- Semantic invariant: A mounted animal is prepared, compared, marked, and ordered within a race or riding system.
- Surface relation: indirect; roots occurring in measuring, following, nearness, marking, and holding disclose a detailed equine scene.
- Surprising reach: The channel distinguishes paired racers, the second horse, a controlled trot, hidden white markings, fetlock restraint, and saddle wood.

#### Subchannel A. Race Pair, Pursuit, and Second Place
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Two horses run side by side, one leads, and the following horse occupies the named second position.
- Active motifs: horse matched against horse `ك ي ل:B004/m01`; racing run `ع د و:B002/m01`; second horse following the leader `ص ل ي:B007/m01`; prepared riding horse `ق ر ب:B013/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ك ي ل` 83:2,3; `ع د و` 83:12; `ص ل ي` 83:16; `ق ر ب` 83:21,28.
- Synthesis: Matching establishes the pair, running realizes the contest, and following converts spatial relation into ranked finish order.

#### Subchannel B. Controlled Trot and Comparative Gait
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A rider brings a horse into a measured gait and compares its pace with another mount.
- Active motifs: controlled equine trot `ق ر ب:B014/m01`; horse-to-horse comparison `ك ي ل:B004/m01`; matching like with like `ن ظ ر:B006/m01`; balanced alignment `و ز ن:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ق ر ب` 83:21,28; `ك ي ل` 83:2,3; `ن ظ ر` 83:23,35; `و ز ن` 83:3.
- Synthesis: Gait is made assessable by alignment. The two mounts occupy comparable courses, allowing rhythm, pace, and relative performance to be judged.

#### Subchannel C. Coat Marks, Legs, and Soundness
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A horse is inspected by the marks and movement of its lower legs before riding or racing.
- Active motifs: hidden white hair on a horse `خ ت م:B006/m01`; branded or colored leg marks `ر ق م:B004/m01`; white or restrained horse legs `م س ك:B011/m01`; lameness shown through the leg `غ م ز:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `خ ت م` 83:25,26; `ر ق م` 83:9,20; `م س ك` 83:26; `غ م ز` 83:30.
- Synthesis: Coat and brand identify the animal, while the way the hoof meets the ground reveals soundness. Visual inspection and locomotion jointly determine readiness.

#### Subchannel D. Saddle Wood and Raised Riding Parts
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Wooden and upright components are assembled into a carrying frame for the mounted animal.
- Active motifs: bare saddle wood `ع ظ م:B009/m01`; carrying implement `ء و ل:B008/m01`; upright structural component `ق و م:B012/m03`; prepared mount with tack `ق ر ب:B013/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ع ظ م` 83:5; `ء و ل` 83:13; `ق و م` 83:6; `ق ر ب` 83:21,28.
- Synthesis: The wooden frame bears load, standing parts stabilize it, and the prepared mount integrates the artifact into transport and sport.

### 18. Settlement, Terrain, Elevation, and Route
- Semantic invariant: Places become inhabitable and navigable through residence, landmark, elevation, boundary, direction, and path.
- Surface relation: indirect; 83:23,35 uses the couch root, 83:27 names Tasnīm, and roots of direction, nearness, and passage occur throughout the surah.
- Surprising reach: Lexical place-making spans a settled city, named mountains and valleys, hot remote steppe, basalt ground, shore edge, overlook, and clear road.

#### Subchannel A. Residence, Settlement, and Agreeable Stay
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: People choose a place, remain there, build habitation, and find the stay agreeable.
- Active motifs: remaining in a place `ء ر ك:B003/m01`; continuing residence `ر ب ب:B007/m01`; established station `ق و م:B006/m01`; agreeable dwelling `ن ع م:B011/m01`; inhabited place `ء ه ل:B004/m01`; city or ordered settlement `د ي ن:B006/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ء ر ك` 83:23,35; `ر ب ب` 83:6,15; `ق و م` 83:6; `ن ع م` 83:22,24; `ء ه ل` 83:31; `د ي ن` 83:11.
- Synthesis: Staying gives duration to location, habitation gives it a human interior, and civic order turns repeated residence into a settlement.

#### Subchannel B. Height, Named Place, and Landmark
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A raised feature becomes visible at distance, receives a name, and guides recognition of the surrounding land.
- Active motifs: elevation `س ن م:B001/m01`; hill or raised ground `س ن م:B004/m01`; named high place `س ن م:B005/m01`; named mountain, valley, or site `ء ر ك:B007/m01`; terrain trace `ر ق م:B005/m01`; visible ridge `ع ر ف:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `س ن م` 83:27; `ء ر ك` 83:23,35; `ر ق م` 83:9,20; `ع ر ف` 83:24.
- Synthesis: Height makes the feature perceptible, naming makes it communicable, and the trace fixes it within a route or remembered landscape.

#### Subchannel C. Remote Desert, Hot Country, and Rough Ground
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A traveler crosses dry, isolated, hot, and physically resistant terrain.
- Active motifs: dry land or desert `ب ر ر:B005/m01`; hot country `ج ر م:B010/m01`; remote settlement or isolated place `ك ف ر:B012/m01`; basalt or lava ground `ح ج ب:B012/m01`; hard uneven ground `ع د و:B010/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ب ر ر` 83:18,22; `ج ر م` 83:29; `ك ف ر` 83:34,36; `ح ج ب` 83:15; `ع د و` 83:12.
- Synthesis: Remoteness limits social refuge, heat and dryness limit water, and hard ground limits movement. The scene is a compound geography of exposure.

#### Subchannel D. Edge, Side, Shore, and Overlook
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An observer reaches a boundary, takes a side position, and looks down or across from an elevated edge.
- Active motifs: shore or projecting edge `ط ف ف:B004/m01`; object's rim or border `ح ج ب:B007/m01`; opposite side or bank `ع د و:B009/m01`; high overlook `و ف ي:B003/m01`; direction or facing side `و ج ه:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ط ف ف` 83:1; `ح ج ب` 83:15; `ع د و` 83:12; `و ف ي` 83:2; `و ج ه` 83:24.
- Synthesis: The border defines where one surface ends, the side locates the observer relative to it, and elevation converts the boundary into an overlook.

#### Subchannel E. Clear Road, Direction, and Passage
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A traveler chooses a direction, enters a clear route, and passes along it without losing the intended destination.
- Active motifs: straight preserving road `ح ف ظ:B007/m01`; visibly clear road `ض ح ك:B005/m01`; deviation from the intended way `ض ل ل:B001/m01`; direction and orientation `و ج ه:B002/m01`; passage and traversal `م ر ر:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ح ف ظ` 83:33; `ض ح ك` 83:29,34; `ض ل ل` 83:32; `و ج ه` 83:24; `م ر ر` 83:30.
- Synthesis: Direction supplies the goal, clarity makes the route readable, and passage realizes movement. Deviation is the contrasting failure that gives straightness practical meaning.

### 19. Dawn, Weather, and Celestial Sign
- Semantic invariant: Changes in light, air, cloud, and celestial position make time and weather visible.
- Surface relation: indirect; 83:5-6,11,34 repeats day, 83:24 brightness, 83:27 Tasnīm, and 83:28 a spring, while lexical branches open sky phenomena.
- Surprising reach: The field connects daybreak with breath, noon with balance, cloud with an encircling crown, lightning with laughter, and an ostrich-shaped lunar mansion.

#### Subchannel A. Daybreak, Sun, and Meridian
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Night cover splits, dawn breathes open, the sun appears, and daylight rises toward noon.
- Active motifs: splitting daybreak `ف ج ر:B002/m01`; dawn opening like breath `ن ف س:B009/m01`; front of the day `و ج ه:B007/m01`; noonday standing `ق و م:B017/m01`; bounded daylight `ي و م:B001/m01`; sun's eye `ع ي ن:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ف ج ر` 83:7; `ن ف س` 83:26; `و ج ه` 83:24; `ق و م` 83:6; `ي و م` 83:5,6,11,34; `ع ي ن` 83:28.
- Synthesis: Dawn splits enclosure, expanding light resembles exhalation, and the sun's rising eye carries the day toward its balanced meridian.

#### Subchannel B. Cloud, Rain, and Lightning
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Cloud gathers and encircles, darkens with rain, releases a heavy shower, and flashes with lightning.
- Active motifs: rain cloud `ر ب ب:B008/m01`; cloud's rain center `ع ي ن:B010/m01`; large rain-bearing cloud `س ق ي:B007/m01`; visible flash `ض ح ك:B006/m01`; encircling cloud crown `ك ل ل:B005/m03`.
- Ayah anchors: `ر ب ب` 83:6,15; `ع ي ن` 83:28; `س ق ي` 83:25; `ض ح ك` 83:29,34; `ك ل ل` 83:12.
- Synthesis: Gathering creates the cloud body, encirclement gives it visible form, rainfall releases its stored water, and lightning briefly reveals the structure from within.

#### Subchannel C. Moist Breeze and Greening Land
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A gentle wind carries moisture across land and supports green growth.
- Active motifs: soft breeze `ن ع م:B009/m01`; wetness and dew `ب ل ل:B001/m01`; smooth gentle movement `ر س ل:B003/m01`; green watered ground `ش ر ب:B014/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ن ع م` 83:22,24; `ب ل ل` 83:14 `بَلْ`; `ر س ل` 83:33; `ش ر ب` 83:28.
- Synthesis: Gentle movement distributes moisture, dew settles it onto surfaces, and the ground records the weather as renewed vegetation.

#### Subchannel D. Star, Lunar Mansion, Halo, and Sky Marker
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A celestial object is recognized by shape and surrounding light and used as an ordered sky marker.
- Active motifs: Heart of the Scorpion star `ق ل ب:B010/m01`; lunar mansion within an encircling crown `ك ل ل:B005/m02`; ostrich-shaped lunar mansion `ن ع م:B007/m05`; witnessing sky sign `ش ه د:B008/m01`; sun's eye `ع ي ن:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ق ل ب` 83:14,31; `ك ل ل` 83:12; `ن ع م` 83:22,24; `ش ه د` 83:21; `ع ي ن` 83:28.
- Synthesis: Shape distinguishes the celestial figure, the halo or crown locates it within surrounding light, and the sign function turns observation into orientation and calendrical order.

### 20. Fire, Heat, Anger, and Armed Conflict
- Semantic invariant: Heat intensifies from kindling to blaze and extends analogically into inflamed emotion and battle.
- Surface relation: direct; 83:16 names entry into the blaze, while 83:7,12,29-36 frames conflict and reversal.
- Surprising reach: The fire complex includes ember hidden in soil, a fire drill, grinding stone, a face heated by anger, rear battle ranks, and remembered sacrilegious wars.

#### Subchannel A. Blaze and Entry into Fire
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: Fire intensifies into a fierce blaze and a person enters or meets its heat.
- Active motifs: fiercely blazing fire `ج ح م:B001/m01`; entering and suffering fire `ص ل ي:B003/m01`; engulfing cover `ك ف ر:B002/m01`; ruin as outcome `و ي ل:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ج ح م` 83:16; `ص ل ي` 83:16; `ك ف ر` 83:34,36; `و ي ل` 83:1,10.
- Synthesis: Engulfment removes distance from the blaze, encounter turns heat into bodily experience, and ruin names the terminal state.

#### Subchannel B. Fire Drill, Ember, and Hearth Stone
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Friction creates a spark, fuel is kindled, heat is preserved beneath earth, and food or material is worked on stone.
- Active motifs: fire drill `ك ي ل:B002/m01`; kindling and heating `ص ل ي:B004/m01`; ember retained in soil `م س ك:B008/m01`; anvil or grinding stone `ع ل و:B009/m01`; incense wood as fuel `ء ل ي:B014/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ك ي ل` 83:2,3; `ص ل ي` 83:16; `م س ك` 83:26; `ع ل و` 83:18,19; `ء ل ي` 83:31 `إِلَىٰ`.
- Synthesis: Friction initiates heat, fuel enlarges it, buried ember preserves it between uses, and the stone provides a stable surface for heat-assisted work.

#### Subchannel C. Inflamed Face and Anger
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Anger heats the face, hardens protective zeal, and may erupt into a blow.
- Active motifs: face burning with anger `ج ح م:B004/m01`; protective anger or zeal `ح ف ظ:B005/m01`; striking the face `و ج ه:B013/m01`; heart injury `ق ل ب:B016/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ج ح م` 83:16; `ح ف ظ` 83:33; `و ج ه` 83:24; `ق ل ب` 83:14,31.
- Synthesis: Heat makes emotion visible, zeal gives it a defended object, and the face-strike converts affect into bodily violence that reaches inward.

#### Subchannel D. Battle Line, Clash, and Victory
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Forces arrange a front and rear, meet in combat, test the truth of an assault, and produce domination or victory.
- Active motifs: battle at its fiercest `ج ح م:B002/m01`; rear rank `ك ي ل:B003/m01`; armed resistance `ق و م:B014/m01`; assault proving true or false `ك ذ ب:B004/m01`; conquest and dominance `ع ل و:B004/m01`; victory and prevailing height `ب ر ر:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ج ح م` 83:16; `ك ي ل` 83:2,3; `ق و م` 83:6; `ك ذ ب` 83:10-12,17; `ع ل و` 83:18,19; `ب ر ر` 83:18,22.
- Synthesis: Formation organizes the forces, clash tests their action, and the result separates failed charge from successful domination.

#### Subchannel E. Remembered Battle Day and Sacrilege
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A violation of sanctity becomes a named historical battle and is remembered as a calamitous day.
- Active motifs: battles of al-Fijār `ف ج ر:B006/m01`; calamitous day-event `ي و م:B003/m01`; tribal name within the conflict memory `ج ر م:B011/m01`; overwhelming ordeal `ع ظ م:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ف ج ر` 83:7; `ي و م` 83:5,6,11,34; `ج ر م` 83:29; `ع ظ م` 83:5.
- Synthesis: Sacrilege supplies the cause, battle supplies the event, tribal naming supplies participants, and the remembered day preserves the conflict as a moralized history.

### 21. Enclosure, Access, and Built Interior
- Semantic invariant: An inside is made by covering, sealing, guarding, furnishing, or imprisoning, and its meaning depends on who may enter or leave.
- Surface relation: direct; 83:7-8 names confinement, 83:15 exclusion, 83:23,35 couches, and 83:25 sealing.
- Surprising reach: The enclosure field joins veil, prison, curtained bedchamber, canopy, oven lid, brick kiln, and blindness.

#### Subchannel A. Veil, Barrier, and Secured Access
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A covering blocks approach and a seal stabilizes the boundary against entry.
- Active motifs: prevention of access `ح ج ب:B001/m01`; physical screen or veil `ح ج ب:B002/m01`; secured seal `خ ت م:B003/m01`; covering layer `ك ف ر:B001/m01`; prohibition or prevention `ء ل ي:B009/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ح ج ب` 83:15; `خ ت م` 83:25,26; `ك ف ر` 83:34,36; `ء ل ي` 83:31 `إِلَىٰ`.
- Synthesis: The screen creates separation, prevention gives it normative force, and sealing makes the blocked condition durable.

#### Subchannel B. Prison, Holding, and Entrapment
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A person or object is seized, retained in a fixed place, and denied an exit.
- Active motifs: confinement in prison `س ج ن:B001/m01`; holding and detention `م س ك:B001/m01`; capture and attachment `ب ل ل:B004/m01`; inescapable entrapment `ر ي ن:B002/m01`; fixed station `ق و م:B006/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `س ج ن` 83:7,8; `م س ك` 83:26; `ب ل ل` 83:14 `بَلْ`; `ر ي ن` 83:14; `ق و م` 83:6.
- Synthesis: Seizure initiates confinement, a fixed place supplies its setting, and inability to escape converts temporary holding into entrapment.

#### Subchannel C. Curtained Couch and Furnished Chamber
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A decorated couch stands inside a curtained chamber beneath an enclosing canopy.
- Active motifs: decorated couch in a curtained chamber `ء ر ك:B004/m01`; canopy or curtained enclosure `ك ل ل:B006/m01`; furniture leg or upright support `ق و م:B012/m03`; chamberlain regulating entrance `ح ج ب:B003/m01`; room as a drinking or domestic space `ش ر ب:B004/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: `ء ر ك` 83:23,35; `ك ل ل` 83:12; `ق و م` 83:6; `ح ج ب` 83:15; `ش ر ب` 83:28.
- Synthesis: Upright supports carry the couch and canopy, curtains create privacy, and the chamberlain gives the furnished interior a controlled social threshold.

#### Subchannel D. Oven Lid and Brick Kiln
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A heat-resistant enclosure is covered for cooking or firing bricks.
- Active motifs: oven lid `و ف ي:B005/m01`; brick-firing house `و ف ي:B005/m02`; covering barrier `ح ج ب:B002/m01`; standing structural part `ق و م:B012/m03`; heated working `ص ل ي:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `و ف ي` 83:2; `ح ج ب` 83:15; `ق و م` 83:6; `ص ل ي` 83:16.
- Synthesis: Walls and standing parts contain heat, the lid regulates the opening, and firing transforms the enclosed material into cooked food or brick.

#### Subchannel E. Occluded but Intact Eye
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A visual organ remains structurally present while its access to light is covered.
- Active motifs: blindness as occlusion `ح ج ب:B009/m01`; intact sightless eye `ق و م:B021/m01`; obscuring cover `ك ف ر:B001/m01`; hiddenness from perception `ض ل ل:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ح ج ب` 83:15; `ق و م` 83:6; `ك ف ر` 83:34,36; `ض ل ل` 83:32.
- Synthesis: The eye is an interior whose aperture no longer functions. Covering blocks the incoming path while leaving the organ itself in place.

### 22. Material Craft, Clothing, and Adornment
- Semantic invariant: Raw wood, fiber, hide, pigment, stone, metal, and fragrance are transformed into functional or status-bearing objects.
- Surface relation: indirect; sealing, inscription, color, musk, couches, and drink vessels provide surface anchors for a broad lexical craft field.
- Surprising reach: The craft field includes tent poles, bier, saddle wood, twisted rope, deceptive cloth, bracelet, coronation crown, pure gold, aloeswood incense, anvil, spear tip, and grinding slab.

#### Subchannel A. Wooden Support, Carrier, and Frame
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A woodworker shapes rigid pieces into supports, carrying frames, furniture, and striking tools.
- Active motifs: tent pole or structural tool `ء و ل:B008/m02`; bier or carrying frame `ء و ل:B008/m03`; saddle wood `ع ظ م:B009/m01`; furniture support `ق و م:B012/m03`; wooden striking club `ق و ل:B008/m01`; worked wooden cup `ن ض ر:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ء و ل` 83:13; `ع ظ م` 83:5; `ق و م` 83:6; `ق و ل` 83:13,17,32; `ن ض ر` 83:24.
- Synthesis: Shaping gives wood a load-bearing form, joining turns pieces into a frame, and the same material logic produces domestic support, transport, riding equipment, and hand tool.

#### Subchannel B. Rope Twisting, Joining, and Tether
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Fibers are joined, twisted under tension, and made into a rope capable of securing an animal or load.
- Active motifs: tightly twisted rope `م ر ر:B005/m01`; joining components `ك ت ب:B001/m01`; neck tether `ش ر ب:B008/m01`; holding fast `م س ك:B001/m01`; upright point of attachment `ق و م:B012/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `م ر ر` 83:30; `ك ت ب` 83:7,9,18,20; `ش ر ب` 83:28; `م س ك` 83:26; `ق و م` 83:6.
- Synthesis: Joining creates length, twisting creates strength, and a fixed support converts tensile material into effective restraint.

#### Subchannel C. Cloth, Dye, and Pattern
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Cloth receives liquid color and patterned surface treatment that may reveal or disguise its quality.
- Active motifs: garment or covering cloth `ث و ب:B003/m01`; pouring dye into cloth `س ق ي:B009/m01`; color absorbed into material `ش ر ب:B007/m01`; embroidered pattern `ر ق م:B003/m01`; cloth deceptive in appearance `ك ذ ب:B009/m01`; clear material color `ج ر م:B009/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ث و ب` 83:36; `س ق ي` 83:25; `ش ر ب` 83:28; `ر ق م` 83:9,20; `ك ذ ب` 83:10-12,17; `ج ر م` 83:29.
- Synthesis: Dye enters the fibers, pattern organizes the surface, and the finished appearance may either display material quality or falsely imply it.

#### Subchannel D. Bracelet, Crown, and Pure Metal
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Refined metal is shaped into wrist ornament and a head-covering emblem of rank.
- Active motifs: bracelet `ق ل ب:B008/m01`; wrist bangle `م س ك:B006/m01`; encircling crown `ك ل ل:B005/m01`; coronation crown `ك ف ر:B015/m01`; pure bright gold `ن ض ر:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ق ل ب` 83:14,31; `م س ك` 83:26; `ك ل ل` 83:12; `ك ف ر` 83:34,36; `ن ض ر` 83:24.
- Synthesis: Refinement supplies the metal, circular shaping produces bracelet and crown, and bodily placement distinguishes personal ornament from public insignia of rank.

#### Subchannel E. Perfume, Camphor, and Aloeswood Incense
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Aromatic wood and camphor are prepared or burned so that fragrance marks a body, room, or ceremonial setting.
- Active motifs: aloeswood incense `ء ل ي:B014/m01`; camphor fragrance `ك ف ر:B011/m01`; recognizing and applying scent `ع ر ف:B004/m01`; smoldering fuel `ص ل ي:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ء ل ي` 83:31 `إِلَىٰ`; `ك ف ر` 83:34,36; `ع ر ف` 83:24; `ص ل ي` 83:16.
- Synthesis: Aromatic material is selected, heat releases its scent, and smell extends the crafted object beyond its physical boundary into the surrounding space.

#### Subchannel F. Anvil, Spear Tip, and Grinding Slab
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Hard stone or metal provides a resistant surface for pounding, sharpening, and forming tools.
- Active motifs: anvil or raised stone `ع ل و:B009/m01`; spear point `ع ل و:B009/m02`; grinding slab `ص ل ي:B009/m01`; hard uneven ground as resistant material `ع د و:B010/m01`; pointed cutting implement `د ر ي:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ع ل و` 83:18,19; `ص ل ي` 83:16; `ع د و` 83:12; `د ر ي` 83:8,19.
- Synthesis: Resistance makes pounding possible, the slab receives repeated impact, and sharpening concentrates force into an edge or point.

#### Subchannel G. Blade, Handle, Sheath, and Point
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An edged weapon is assembled from blade and standing handle, sharpened at its point, and stored in a fitted sheath.
- Active motifs: sword or knife sheath `ق ر ب:B010/m01`; standing sword component `ق و م:B012/m02`; sword's projecting lip or edge `ش ر ب:B006/m02`; pointed implement `د ر ي:B004/m01`; spear point `ع ل و:B009/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: `ق ر ب` 83:21,28; `ق و م` 83:6; `ش ر ب` 83:28; `د ر ي` 83:8,19; `ع ل و` 83:18,19.
- Synthesis: Handle controls the blade, sharpening concentrates force at the edge or point, and the sheath encloses the finished weapon between uses.

### 23. Dispatch, Passage, Return, and Navigation
- Semantic invariant: Motion is organized by activation, sending, route, succession, arrival, return, and control of a vehicle.
- Surface relation: mixed; 83:4 names being raised, 83:30 passing by, 83:31 returning, and 83:33 being sent.
- Surprising reach: The movement field reaches from messenger and collective rush to paced travel, a small boat, and its captain.

#### Subchannel A. Rousing, Dispatch, and Messenger
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A still agent is activated, given a direction, and sent as a messenger toward a task.
- Active motifs: rousing from stillness `ب ع ث:B001/m01`; commissioning and dispatch `ب ع ث:B002/m01`; sending forth `ر س ل:B001/m01`; messenger and message `ر س ل:B002/m01`; rising to undertake an affair `ق و م:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ب ع ث` 83:4; `ر س ل` 83:33; `ق و م` 83:6.
- Synthesis: Activation creates readiness, commissioning supplies purpose, and sending converts intention into directed movement carried by an identified messenger.

#### Subchannel B. Passage, Following, and Return
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A traveler passes a point, follows a prior movement, turns, and returns to a former place or condition.
- Active motifs: passage and crossing `م ر ر:B001/m01`; following in succession `ت ل و:B001/m01`; return to a place or state `ث و ب:B001/m01`; turning back `ق ل ب:B005/m01`; movement beyond or around `ع د و:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `م ر ر` 83:30; `ت ل و` 83:13; `ث و ب` 83:36; `ق ل ب` 83:14,31; `ع د و` 83:12.
- Synthesis: Passage marks departure from one point, following supplies order, and turning transforms outward movement into return.

#### Subchannel C. Collective Rush and Scattering
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A group is stirred into rapid motion, moves in waves, and may scatter under pressure.
- Active motifs: collective surge `ب ع ث:B004/m01`; sudden outpouring `ف ج ر:B003/m01`; successive moving groups `ر س ل:B005/m01`; group dispersal `ن ع م:B008/m01`; mixed commotion `ب ل ل:B009/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ب ع ث` 83:4; `ف ج ر` 83:7; `ر س ل` 83:33; `ن ع م` 83:22,24; `ب ل ل` 83:14 `بَلْ`.
- Synthesis: Activation starts the surge, successive waves preserve some order, and scattering marks the point where collective motion exceeds coordination.

#### Subchannel D. Approach, Appointment, and Arrival
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A destination or appointed meeting draws near, the traveler adopts a direction, and participants arrive together.
- Active motifs: approach and nearness `ق ر ب:B001/m01`; approaching time `ق ر ب:B002/m01`; arriving for an appointment `و ف ي:B004/m01`; aiming toward a destination `د ر ي:B002/m01`; directional face `و ج ه:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ق ر ب` 83:21,28; `و ف ي` 83:2; `د ر ي` 83:8,19; `و ج ه` 83:24.
- Synthesis: Direction aligns movement, nearness measures remaining distance or time, and arrival fulfills the appointment by gathering participants at one place.

#### Subchannel E. Small Boat, Captain, and Paced Travel
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A small craft carries passengers under a captain who regulates speed and course.
- Active motifs: small boat `ق ر ب:B011/m01`; captain or master of sailors `ر ب ب:B017/m01`; smooth travel `ر س ل:B003/m01`; deliberate pacing `ر س ل:B004/m01`; high lookout `و ف ي:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ق ر ب` 83:21,28; `ر ب ب` 83:6,15; `ر س ل` 83:33; `و ف ي` 83:2.
- Synthesis: The boat supplies transport, the captain supplies coordinated command, pacing moderates movement, and the lookout converts elevation into navigational awareness.

#### Subchannel F. Repulse, Redirection, and Reversal of Course
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An approaching person or action is turned away, breaks its prior following relation, and takes a reversed course.
- Active motifs: repelling from the face or front `و ج ه:B014/m01`; leaving after accompaniment `ت ل و:B005/m01`; turning a thing from its face `ق ل ب:B004/m01`; meeting an appointment and then separating `و ف ي:B004/m01`; redirected passage `ع د و:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `و ج ه` 83:24; `ت ل و` 83:13; `ق ل ب` 83:14,31; `و ف ي` 83:2; `ع د و` 83:12.
- Synthesis: Repulse blocks forward approach, reversal changes orientation, and abandonment ends the prior relation of following or accompaniment.

### 24. Resolve, Rank, Affect, and Capacity
- Semantic invariant: A person's ability to act is shaped by vigor, composure, fatigue, self-estimation, honor, and comfort.
- Surface relation: mixed; 83:5 calls the day great, 83:18-19 names elevation, 83:22-24 bliss and radiance, and 83:26 competition.
- Surprising reach: Inner condition ranges from patient composure and alertness to helplessness, pride, conquest, bodily softness, and luminous well-being.

#### Subchannel A. Effort, Capacity, and Firm Resolve
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An agent has the capacity to act, exerts effort, rises to the task, and sustains firm resolve.
- Active motifs: strenuous effort `ء ل ي:B010/m01`; capacity and ability `ء ل ي:B011/m01`; undertaking an affair `ق و م:B003/m01`; firm determination `م ر ر:B006/m01`; strength of character `ن ف س:B014/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ء ل ي` 83:31 `إِلَىٰ`; `ق و م` 83:6; `م ر ر` 83:30; `ن ف س` 83:26.
- Synthesis: Capacity makes action possible, effort initiates it, and resolve preserves the undertaking when resistance appears.

#### Subchannel B. Composure, Alertness, and Deliberate Pace
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A person remains inwardly steady, watches carefully, and acts without impulsive haste.
- Active motifs: patient composed self `ع ر ف:B010/m01`; vigilant alertness `ح ف ظ:B004/m01`; gentleness and deliberation `ر س ل:B004/m01`; secure heart `ء م ن:B001/m01`; balanced judgment `و ز ن:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ع ر ف` 83:24; `ح ف ظ` 83:33; `ر س ل` 83:33; `ء م ن` 83:29,34; `و ز ن` 83:3.
- Synthesis: Security quiets fear, alertness preserves attention, and measured judgment lets action proceed at a deliberate pace.

#### Subchannel C. Honor, Awe, and Social Weight
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A person or affair is perceived as great, elevated, and worthy of deference.
- Active motifs: greatness and strength `ع ظ م:B001/m01`; awe before magnitude `ع ظ م:B007/m01`; honor and sanctity `ع ظ م:B010/m01`; elevated rank `ع ل و:B002/m01`; social prestige `و ج ه:B006/m01`; recognized weight or standing `و ز ن:B007/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ع ظ م` 83:5; `ع ل و` 83:18,19; `و ج ه` 83:24; `و ز ن` 83:3.
- Synthesis: Magnitude generates awe, elevation establishes rank, and social weight converts those perceptions into durable prestige.

#### Subchannel D. Pride, Domination, and Overreaching
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Self-magnification becomes arrogance and seeks confirmation through domination over others.
- Active motifs: boastful self-enlargement `ع ظ م:B006/m01`; arrogant elevation `ع ل و:B003/m01`; domination and conquest `ع ل و:B004/m01`; insolent exultation `ف ك ه:B006/m01`; prevailing over another `ب ر ر:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ع ظ م` 83:5; `ع ل و` 83:18,19; `ف ك ه` 83:31; `ب ر ر` 83:18,22.
- Synthesis: Pride changes self-estimation, arrogance places the self above others, and domination attempts to make that imagined hierarchy materially true.

#### Subchannel E. Comfort, Softness, and Radiant Well-Being
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: Ease of condition produces bodily softness, inward delight, recovery, and visible radiance.
- Active motifs: good condition and blessing `ن ع م:B001/m01`; softness and ease `ن ع م:B002/m01`; delightful well-being `ف ك ه:B001/m01`; generous ease `ر س ل:B010/m01`; recovery from illness `ب ل ل:B003/m01`; radiant beauty `ن ض ر:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ن ع م` 83:22,24; `ف ك ه` 83:31; `ر س ل` 83:33; `ب ل ل` 83:14 `بَلْ`; `ن ض ر` 83:24.
- Synthesis: Relief removes hardship, ease settles into bodily softness, delight supplies inward experience, and radiance makes well-being visible on the face.

#### Subchannel F. Fatigue, Helplessness, and Diminished Agency
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Capacity declines through dullness, fatigue, weakness, or smallness until action stalls.
- Active motifs: blunt fatigue `ك ل ل:B001/m01`; helpless weakness `ظ ن ن:B008/m01`; stopping through weariness `ق و م:B016/m01`; slightness or paucity `ط ف ف:B001/m01`; slackened effort `ء ل ي:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ك ل ل` 83:12; `ظ ن ن` 83:4; `ق و م` 83:6; `ط ف ف` 83:1; `ء ل ي` 83:31 `إِلَىٰ`.
- Synthesis: Slight capacity reduces force, fatigue blunts continuation, and helplessness turns a temporary pause into inability to complete the act.

## Standalone Subchannels

### S1. Lot Gambling with Ranked Arrows
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Gaming arrows are kept in a container, drawn by lot, and distinguished by ranked identity and stake tokens.
- Active motifs: leather container for gaming arrows `ر ب ب:B010/m01`; grouped lot arrows `ر ب ب:B010/m02`; fifth gaming arrow `ن ف س:B016/m01`; elevated or favored lot arrow `ع ل و:B009/m03`; smooth nut used as a counting or value token `خ ت م:B007/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: `ر ب ب` 83:6,15; `ن ف س` 83:26; `ع ل و` 83:18,19; `خ ت م` 83:25,26.
- Synthesis: The container organizes the arrows, rank differentiates their outcomes, drawing converts uncertainty into allocation, and the smooth token gives the result a countable stake.

### S2. Qillah Bat-and-Target Play
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A player uses a shaped wooden bat to strike the qillah in a compact recreation scene.
- Active motifs: wooden striking bat `ق و ل:B008/m01`; struck qillah as the bat's target `ق و ل:B008/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: `ق و ل` 83:13,17,32.
- Synthesis: One lexical evidence container preserves both indispensable roles of the game: the hand-held striking tool and the small target it propels.
